# Chapter 35: The Hollow King's Gambit

AMELIA

She wakes to Victor's voice.

"Amelia."

It comes from the doorway. Soft. Tender. Exactly the way he says her name when he thinks she's asleep.

She turns expecting him to be closer than he is but sees him shirtless, looking as if he hasn't slept all night.

"You're awake," he says.

"I was sleeping," she says. Her voice is thick. She blinks. Rubs her face. "What's wrong?"

"Nothing. I just wanted to check on you." He steps into the room. Bare feet on the floorboards. Silent. He sits on the edge of the bed. Close.

She looks at him closely, not believing there isn't anything wrong. Even though he looks calm, his breathing isn't off. There is just something about the way he is standing, shoulders tight, jaw clenched. The way he holds himself still when he's trying not to move.

"You're tense," she says.

"Long day." He doesn't look at her. Looks past her instead. "A lot on my plate."

"I'm here," she says. "I'm always here."

He smiles. Small. Real. "I know."

"I'm learning."

He reaches for her hand. His fingers are warm. Dry. His pulse is steady against her skin. She feels it through the bond, a low, unchanging hum. His presence. Warm. Certain. Alive.

She squeezes his hand. "I'm fine, Victor."

"I'm staying."

He sits down beside her on the bed. Close enough that his shoulder brushes hers. The feel of her shirt on his bare skin, she can feel the heat radiating through her body.

She leans into him. Rests her head on his shoulder. His arm comes around her. Heavy. Secure. She closes her eyes. Breathes in his scent, smoke and leather and something wild. And something wild that just does something to her. She knows that scent is only for her.

His hand moves on her back. Slow circles. Rhythmic. Soothing.

She should be asleep. She should be drifting into the easy sleep she gets when he's near. But she knows that something is going to happen. And happen soon.

The room is quiet. Too quiet. No sounds from the compound outside. No crickets. No wind. No distant pack sounds. Just silence. A silence so complete it feels like pressure against her ears.

Her eyes snap open.

Victor's arm is still around her. His hand is still moving on her back. His breathing is still slow and even. But his heartbeat is wrong.

It's too slow.

Too steady.

No rhythm. No pulse. Just a metronome tick tick tick. Mechanical. Empty.

She tenses. Her breath catches.

"Amelia."

Victor's voice. From above her. Not from beside her.

She looks up. His face is inches from hers. Eyes gray. But not his gray. His eyes are warm, like storm clouds. These are dark. Like a pond in winter. Cold. Empty.

"Shh," he says. His voice is Victor's. Exactly Victor's. But something is wrong. The cadence is off. The warmth is gone. It's a mask. Perfect. Hollow.

She tries to pull away. His grip tightens. Not painful. Not threatening. Just firm. Unyielding.

"You're dreaming," he says. "You've always dreamed. You've always imagined me. There is no bond. There is no pack. There is no Victor Slade. You are alone. You have always been alone."

The room flickers. The walls dissolve into mist. The bed dissolves. She is standing in a dark forest, naked, cold, terrified. Victor stands in front of her. His arms at his sides. His eyes empty. His mouth a flat line.

"Victor?" she says. Her voice is trembling.

He does not answer. He just watches her. Empty eyes. Empty face. Empty.

"You were never bonded," he says. "You never will be. The bond is a lie. The Moon Goddess is a lie. Everything is a lie. You are a human woman standing in the dark, and no one is coming for you."

The darkness closes in. Trees around her. Vines. Roots. Closing in. She can't move. Can't breathe. Can't think. The voice, Victor's voice, but not Victor, continues to speak. Soft. Steady. Relentless.

"You are alone. You have always been alone. You will die alone. And no one will even know you were gone."

The darkness presses against her. The vines tighten. Her chest burns. Her lungs burn. Her vision blurs.

And then, a warmth. A pulse. A presence.

The bond.

Not Victor's voice. Not Victor's face. The bond itself. Certain. Warm. Unshakable. It pushes back against the darkness. Not a wall. Not a barrier. A hand. Reaching for hers across the dark.

She grabs it. Clutches it with everything she has. And holds on.

She wakes up all of a sudden, desperate for air. The darkness screams. The vines dissolve. The forest vanishes. She is on the bed, heart hammering. Sweat soaking her shirt. Victor standing above her. Real Victor. Gray eyes wide. Mouth open. Hand reaching for her face.

"Amelia. Amelia. Look at me."

She looks. Tears are on her cheeks. She doesn't remember crying. She doesn't remember falling. She remembers the darkness. She remembers the voice. She remembers the vines closing in.

"It wasn't real," she says. Her voice is a whisper. "Was it?"

"No." He pulls her against his chest. Her head on his shoulder. His arms locked around her. "No. It wasn't real. I'm here. I'm right here."

"You felt it?" she says.

"I felt it. The Hollow King whispered through the bond. He tried to — " He stops. Swallows. "He's trying to break us. From the outside."

She closes her eyes. Breathing hard. Her hands grip his shirt. Fingers twisting in fabric. "I saw you. You were hollowed. Your eyes — they were empty."

"I know." His voice is tight. "I felt you seeing me. I felt the fear. I felt the darkness closing in." His arms tighten. "Amelia. Listen to me. I am not hollowed. I am alive. I am here. And I am not letting go."

"I know," she says. "I know. I felt the bond. It pushed back."

"It always will." He pulls back. Looks at her. Gray eyes steady. Face pale from the effort of what he just did. "I felt him reach for you through the bond. I felt him pull. I had to — I had to reach back. Harder. I don't know how he did it, but he's in the bond now. He's looking for a crack. He's looking for a way in."

"Can he get through?"

"Not if I hold the door shut." He presses his forehead to hers. "But he won't stop. The Hollow King doesn't attack when he knows he can't win. He attacks when he knows he can't win head-on. He'll keep coming. He'll keep whispering. He'll keep finding weaknesses."

"Like the fear that I'm alone," she says.

"Exactly like that."

She looks at him. Really looks at him. He's shaking. Not visibly. He just pushed back a Void-Eater's mind whisper through the bond, and his hands are still trembling.

"You okay?" she says.

"I'm fine." He's lying. They both know it's a lie. His breathing is uneven. His jaw is clenched. His eyes are tired. He's exhausted. He just did something he's never done before — he reached through the bond not for himself, but for her. For protection. For defense. And it took something from him.

She touches his face. Thumbs pressing against his cheekbones. His skin is cold. Sweat-slick.

"You don't have to hold the door," she says. "Let me help."

He looks at her. Gray eyes wide. Vulnerable. A look she has never seen on him.

"I can't risk it," he says.

"You didn't risk it. You did it. You protected me." She leans up. Kisses his cheek. "I want to protect you too. Let me."

He closes his eyes. Nods. Once. When he opens them, the vulnerability is gone. Replaced by something harder. Something darker.

"Tonight, I stay awake," he says. "You sleep. If he comes back, I'll be ready."

She doesn't argue. She knows he's right. She can't sleep, not after that. Not with the Hollow King's whispers still echoing in her ears. So she stays awake. Beside him. Her head on his chest. His arm around her. The bond between them.

She drifts back off to sleep.

---

VICTOR

He stays awake. There is no way he feels he will get rest tonight.

Amelia's breathing starts to even out within minutes. Her body starts to relax against his chest. Her fingers slowly uncurl from his shirt and her face relaxes, then she drifts peacefully off to sleep.

The bond is a low hum that is warm, but beneath that is something else. Something that feels cold.

He knows he cannot keep this up all night. But he doesn't see any other way. But if the Hollow King comes back, he needs to be ready. He needs to hold the door shut. Again. And again. Until dawn.

His hand moves on Amelia's back. Slow circles. Rhythmic. Soothing. The same rhythm he used before. He doesn't notice it. It's automatic. Protective. He has been touching her like this since the first time he held her. Before the bond. Before the ceremony. Before any of it.

His mind replays the attack. The Hollow King's voice. The way it sounded like him. The way Amelia's fear hit the bond, sharp, sudden, like a knife. He felt it. Felt it in his own chest. Felt it like his heart had been ripped out.

He has never felt anything like that. The bond was supposed to be a bridge. A connection. He thought he understood what it meant to feel her, her warmth, her certainty, her stubborn refusal to be frightened. But he did not understand. Not really. Not until the Hollow King reached for her and he realized that her pain was his pain. Her fear was his fear. Her loss was his loss. The bond is not a bridge. It is a wound. An open, bleeding wound that connects two people forever.

And the Hollow King knows it.

He shifts slightly. Amelia stirs. Mumbles. Her hand finds his chest. Fingers pressing against his skin. Her breathing stays even. She is still asleep. Thank the Moon Goddess.

He looks at her. In the dim light from the hallway, she looks peaceful. Her lips are slightly parted. Her dark hair is spread across the pillow. Her face is soft. No tension. No fear. Nothing but sleep.

She was so terrified. He can still feel it through the bond, her fear, sharp and sudden. But beneath that fear is something else. Something he didn't expect. Not panic. Not flight. Something stronger.

She fought back.

She grabbed the bond and held on. With everything she had. No training. No preparation. No idea what she was doing. Just instinct. A human woman fighting a Void-Eater's mind whisper with nothing but a bond and a will to survive.

He is so proud of her.

The thought surprises him. He doesn't get proud. He gets angry. He gets frustrated. He gets worried. He does not get proud. But he is proud. Of her. Of Amelia.

He drops his forehead against hers. Closes his eyes. Breathes her in. Her scent is mixed with sweat and fear and sleep, but underneath it all, the same soft smell she always has. Something like honey and herbs and morning light.

The bond hums. He can still feel the Hollow King on the other side, faint, like a cold draft in a warm room. He braces against it. Holds the door shut. Waits for dawn.

---

AMELIA

She wakes before dawn.

Victor's arm is still around her. Her head is still on his chest. His heartbeat is steady beneath her ear. Strong. Real. Alive.

She is still alone. But she does not feel it.

The Hollow King's whispers have faded. The darkness is gone. The forest is gone. There is only the room. The bed. Victor's arm. The bond.

But something has changed. She can feel the Hollow King on the other side of the bond, faint, cold, watching. Like a shadow at the edge of a room. He is still there. Still trying. Still looking for a crack.

She presses her face into Victor's chest. Breathing him in. She presses harder against him. Her hand finds his. Fingers lacing with his.

"Stay," she whispers.

"I'm not going anywhere." His voice is thick with sleep. He shifts. Pulls her closer.

She closes her eyes. But she does not sleep. She listens to his heartbeat. And waits for dawn.

---

VICTOR

Dawn comes slowly. Pale light filters through the windows. The compound wakes up. Footsteps in the corridors. Voices in the kitchens. Pack members beginning their day.

Victor does not move. He has been awake since Amelia fell asleep. The Hollow King's whisper has not come back. The door is shut.

But he can still feel the cold on the other side. The Hollow King is patient. He will come back. He will keep coming back. And every time, Victor will have to hold the door shut. And the question is: how long can he hold it?

Amelia shifts. Wakes. Her eyes open. Gray light reflecting in them. She looks at him. Smiles. Sleepy. Soft. Real.

"You stayed," she says.

"I told you I would."

She reaches up. Touches his face. Thumbs pressing against his cheekbones. He is tired. She can see it. Dark circles under his eyes. Paleness in his skin.

"You look terrible," she says.

"I feel terrible." He catches her wrist. Pulls her hand to his lips. Kisses her palm. "You?"

"I'm fine. Scared. But fine."

He nods.

"I need to talk to Josiah," he says. "About the bond. About how to protect it."

Josiah knows the old ways. The old magic. He has done this before, protected bonds from the Hollow King's attacks. He has to have a strategy.

Amelia nods. "I'll come with you."

"No. You stay here. Rest. I don't want — I don't want him coming back while you're distracted."

She studies him. Eyes meeting eyes. "You're worried about me."

"I'm worried about us."

She doesn't argue. She knows he's right. She watches him get up. Dress. Leave the room. She lets it go. Reclines against the pillow. Closes her eyes.

And waits.

---

AMELIA

She does not sleep.

The Hollow King is still on the other side of the bond. She can feel him, cold, distant, watching. He is patient. He will come back. He will keep coming back.

She lies in bed. Staring at the ceiling. Thinking about the bond. Thinking about what just happened. The Hollow King didn't attack Victor's body. He didn't attack the compound. He attacked the connection between her and Victor, the bond. The bridge. The thing that connects them.

And he got close. Too close. Amelia felt his whispers in her mind. Felt the darkness closing in. Felt the vines. The cold. The emptiness. She would have broken. If not for the bond. If not for Victor reaching back.

The bond is not just a bridge. It's a weapon. A barrier. A shield. And the Hollow King knows it. That's why he's attacking it. That's why he's targeting the connection between Victor and Amelia. Because the bond is the pack's strongest defense, and the Hollow King cannot breach it from the outside.

So he tries from the inside. He tries to find cracks. Weak spots. Doubts. Fears. And he found one, Amelia's fear of being alone. The oldest, deepest fear she has carried since she was a child. The feeling of being invisible. Of being unloved. Of being a mistake.

He used that fear against her. And it almost worked.

But it didn't. Not because the bond was strong. Not because Victor was strong. Because she was strong. She grabbed the bond and held on. With everything she had. No training. No preparation. Just instinct. Just love. Just refusal.

She sits up. Pulls her knees to her chest. Looks at her hands. They are still tingling. She thinks about what Josiah told her about the bond. About how it grows stronger with intimacy. About how it's not just magic, it's trust. It's choice. It's two people deciding, every day, to stay together.

The Hollow King can attack her fear. He can attack her doubt. He can attack her loneliness. But he cannot attack her choice. She chose Victor. Not because the bond made her. Not because the Moon Goddess willed it. Because she chose him. And that choice is hers. Nobody can take it from her. Not the Hollow King. Not Aurela. Not anyone.

She closes her eyes. Breathes. And waits for Victor to come back.

---

VICTOR

Josiah is in the war room. Sitting at the head of the table. Staff planted in the floor. Eyes closed. Calm. The way he always is.

Victor sits across from him. Perseus stands by the window. Arms crossed. Face unreadable.

"It started last night," Victor says. "When I fell asleep. The Hollow King whispered to Amelia. Through the bond. He tried to — he broke through."

Josiah opens his eyes. "I know."

"You know?"

"The bond stretches. It connects two minds. The Hollow King is a predator of the mind. Of course he found it."

Victor grips the edge of the table. Knuckles white. "How do I protect her? How do I protect us? He used her fear — her fear of being alone — and I don't — I don't know how to stop him."

"You cannot stop him," Josiah says. Calm. Steady. "Not completely. The Hollow King will always find cracks. He will always find doubts. That is his nature. But you can strengthen the bond. You can make it so strong that his whispers cannot penetrate. So real that his lies cannot take root."

"How?"

"Intimacy," Josiah says. Simple. Direct. "Physical intimacy. Emotional intimacy. The more connected you are — physically, emotionally, spiritually — the stronger the bond becomes. The Hollow King attacks from the outside. But the bond is built from the inside. From trust. From love. From choice."

Victor looks at Perseus. Perseus is watching him. Arms crossed. Face unreadable.

"You're asking me to sleep with her," Victor says. His voice is flat.

"I'm asking you to be honest about what you're doing," Josiah says. "You are already sleeping with her. The question is not whether the intimacy helps — the evidence is right in front of you. The question is whether you are using that intimacy to strengthen the bond, or whether you are treating it as separate from the magic."

Victor looks down. At his hands. At the table. At the grain in the wood.

Josiah is right. He has been sleeping with Amelia. Since the ceremony. Since the bond opened. He has treated it as separate from the magic, as personal. Private. Intimate in a way that has nothing to do with the Hollow King or the pack or the bond itself. He has kept his feelings for her locked up, even though the bond was wide open between them.

"Then do it," Josiah says. "Be honest. With her. With yourself. With the bond. Use every connection you have. Every touch. Every kiss. Every moment of vulnerability. That is how you build a wall the Hollow King cannot breach."

Victor stands. Nods. Once. Turns to leave.

"Victor."

He stops. Looks back.

"Do not rush this," Josiah says. "The Hollow King attacks fear and doubt. Do not let fear and doubt dictate your intimacy. Make it about trust. About love. About choice."

Victor nods. Leaves the war room.

The corridor is empty. The compound is quiet. Dawn has fully broken. Pack members moving through the halls. Greeting each other. Beginning their day.

Victor walks toward the quarters. Slow. Steady. He can feel Amelia waiting. He can feel her fear. Her loneliness. Her strength. The way she fought back. The way she held on.

He reaches the door. Stops. Takes a breath. Opens it.

Amelia is sitting on the bed. Knees to her chest. Eyes on the door. Her face is pale. Her hands are clenched. But her eyes are steady. Gray. Determined. Ready.

"He was there," she says. "Last night. The Hollow King. He whispered to me."

"I know."

"He said you were hollowed. That you were gone. That I was alone."

Victor crosses the room. Sits beside her. Close. "I was not gone."

"I know." Her voice is a whisper. "But I believed it. For a second. For a terrible, terrible second, I believed it."

He reaches for her hand. She lets him. Fingers lacing with his.

"I am not gone," he says. "I am here. I am alive. And I am not letting go."

She looks at him. Eyes wet. Her jaw is set. Her breath is even. She is holding herself together.

"I need you," she says. Simple. Direct. Honest. "I need to feel you. Here. Now. Real. Not a whisper. Not a dream. You."

He understands. The Hollow King attacked through the bond. He reached for her fear. He made her doubt. And now she needs the antidote — not words. Not reassurance. Physical. Real. Present. She needs to feel the bond from the inside. Not through his whispers. Through his touch.

"Me?" he says.

"You," she says. "Now."

He pulls her against him. Her body against his. Her head on his chest. His arm around her waist. Her leg over his. He presses his forehead to hers. Closes his eyes.

"Stay," she says.

"I'm not going anywhere."



She kisses him slowly at first, testing to see if he'll dissolve into mist. He kisses her back with a tenderness that cracks something open in her chest, his hands sliding under her shirt, warm palms against bare skin. She tugs her shirt over her head and throws it aside. He watches her, eyes soft—no hunger, no desperation, just... her. She reaches for the buttons of his jeans, slow and deliberate, each button, each movement, grounding them both.

He sits back against the headboard and pulls her into his lap, her straddling him, legs wrapping around his waist, her bare back to his chest. His hands find hers and press them flat against his chest—over his heart, feeling the beat, steady and real. "Feel that?" he whispers. "That's you. It's always you."

She turns in his grip and faces him, straddling him, knees bracketing the bed on either side of his hips. Her hands slide up his chest, over his shoulders, down his arms, then back up with fingers tangling in his hair, tilting his face to hers. The kiss is deep, slow, searching—she's looking for the Hollow King's ghost in his mouth, but she finds only him. Only Victor. Warm. Present. Alive.

She reaches between them, finds him already hard and waiting, and wraps her fingers around the base. She strokes once—slow, deliberate—and his jaw tightens, eyes closing. "Amelia—," he starts, but she doesn't let him finish. She lines him up at her entrance and slowly lowers herself, one inch, then two. The stretch is immediate, burning. She stops, bows her head, rests her forehead against his, and breathes—letting him in, letting herself in.

"Keep going," he whispers.

She settles down all the way. Her body takes him, walls clamping around him as his hands find her hips—not pushing, just holding, anchoring. She lifts up, slow and deliberate, then sinks back down. A soft gasp escapes her. He groans, a low broken sound, but he doesn't rush, letting her set the pace. One circle. Two. His hands slide up to her waist, fingers tracing the curve of her ribs. "You feel incredible," he says. "Every inch. Every second."

She leans forward, mouth on his neck, teeth on his collarbone. "Don't stop," she whispers.

He moves, slow at first, building. His hands find hers again, pressing them to his chest. "Feel that? It's yours. It's always been yours." She feels it—the beat of his heart, accelerating, matched to the rhythm of her hips. Her hands grip his shoulders, nails digging in, not from pain but from feeling, from the sheer weight of being this close. This real.

"Look at me," he says.

She lifts her head, brown eyes meeting amber. The bond flares wide open between them—not a flood but a floodgate opening, slow and inevitable. She feels his love before she feels his words, feels his pride, his fear, his certainty, and she gives him everything back.

He groans and the rhythm changes, faster, deeper. She meets him, each thrust a promise, each circle a vow. "I'm close," she breathes. "Victor—." His hands slide up, cup her face, thumbs brushing her cheekbones. "Let go," he says. "I've got you. I've always got you."

She does. Her body convulses, a soft broken sound escaping as her walls clamp around him, nails digging into his shoulders, thighs trembling. He holds on barely, her warmth pushing him to the edge. He thrusts once, twice, three times, and breaks. Hips slamming against hers, cock pulsing inside her—hot, thick, spilling into her. A low groan. His forehead drops to her shoulder, his body locking, shaking.

She holds him, arms wrapping around his neck, pulling him close as his weight presses into her—warm, heavy, alive. She feels his heartbeat against her chest, steady now, slowing, matching hers. "I'm here," she whispers. "I'm right here."

The compound is quiet outside. The Hollow King's whispers have faded. The bond is stronger now. Stronger than before. The wall is holding.

He presses his face into her hair. She holds him. His arms wrap around her waist, pulling her flush against him. They breathe together, heartbeats slow from frantic to steady.

She does not let go, holding him close as his head rests on her shoulder, her fingers running through his hair, his breath warm against her skin.

But she knows this is not the end. The Hollow King is still on the other side. Still watching. Still waiting. He will come back. He will keep coming back. And they will keep holding the door shut.

Together.

---

AMELIA

Morning stretches into afternoon. They lie in bed. Not sleeping. Not talking. Just existing. Her head on his chest. His arm around her waist. His leg tangled with hers.

The Hollow King's whispers have faded. The cold is gone. The bond is whole. No scars. No cracks. Just warmth. Just certainty. Just them.

She feels the change. She did not expect it. The intimacy strengthened the bond, Josiah was right. Physical intimacy. Emotional intimacy. Honest. Vulnerable. Unafraid. The bond is stronger now. Wider. Deeper. It feels like the space between them has expanded, not physically, but emotionally. Spiritually. The connection is no longer a thread. It is a river. Flowing. Alive. Unstoppable.

And the Hollow King cannot get through it. Not like before. Not with a whisper. Not with fear. Not with doubt. The bond is too strong. Too real. Too rooted in something he cannot touch.

Trust.

Love.

Choice.

She closes her eyes. Breathes. Lets the warmth wash over her. She drifts into sleep.

Together.



\pagebreak

# Chapter 36: The Sacrifice

AMELIA

She wakes to the bond screaming.

She isn't feeling really any pain. It feels more like a pull of some sort. Like something is being torn out of her chest and there is no way to stop it. Victor is sleeping peacefully beside her. His breathing is calm and steady. But the bond feels like a rope that is being pulled beyond its breaking point, yanking her forward, away from him, and away from everything.

She sits up out of breath, grasping for the sheets of her bed, her chest feeling like it is on fire.

The bond pulls again. Harder. Her chest tightens.

Victor stirs. "Amelia?"

"It's happening," she says. "Something — something is pulling at the bond."

He sits up. Gray eyes wide. Alert. Sleep gone in an instant. "I can feel it too."

The bond jerks. She can feel a dark and cold presence on the other side of the pack wards. The Hollow King. He's testing them, pressing against the boundary with his whole weight.

She gasps. Her knees buckle. Victor catches her.

"The wards," he says. "He's testing them. But he's not — " He stops. His face goes pale. "He's not trying to break in. He's trying to draw me out. If I shift and fight him outside the wards, he has the advantage. He's strong enough to break them. He knows it. I know it. He's making me choose."

"Choose what?"

He looks at her. Gray eyes hard. "Stay inside and let the pack feel the full weight of his assault, or come outside and fight him where he can reach us both through the bond. He's forcing me to sacrifice one of us to save the other. And the answer is obvious. I'd rather take the hit than let him touch you."

She grabs his arms. "No. You don't have to choose. There's a third option."

He looks at her. "No, there isn't."

"There is. The ceremony. The Moon Goddess. Josiah said the bond is strongest when it's honest. When it's open. When there are no walls." She breathes. She thinks about it. The bond keeps pulling. The Hollow King is outside. She can feel him, the coldness, the hunger, ancient. He's nothing wearing a shape. "What if we don't hold it back? What if we let it out? The bond itself. Through both of us. Not as a door you hold shut. As a wall."

He stares at her. For a long moment, he doesn't speak. The bond pulls again. She can hear the Hollow King now, It almost sounds like the whisper in the wind, or of static. Maybe even laughter. He is patient. He is strong. He is waiting for Victor to step outside the wards.

"The Mate Bond can create a barrier?" Victor says. He takes a moment, thinking it over. "Not just connect two people?" Shaking his head. "I don't know. Josiah has never — "

"Then make it work," she says. "You said it yourself. You have to choose. Stay inside or go outside. But if you channel the bond through yourself, make yourself a conduit, and I do the same from inside the bond, maybe we won't have to choose."

He looks at her, really looks at her. Jaw tight, with the realization that she has given him an option he did not expect. An option that costs something, probably everything, but does not sacrifice her.

"If I do this," he says carefully, "I cannot control how much power flows through me. I don't know if I will be able to survive it."

"You don't know if I can survive it either," she says. "I'm human."

He grabs her arms, squeezing gently. "Amelia. Please. Let me — "

"No." She pulls his hands to her face. Places her palms against his cheeks, caressing his face. "We do this together. Both of us. Or not at all."

The bond pulls at them again. They know that the Hollow King is still out there. Still waiting. But the pull is now changing. There is a shift outside like things are coming into focus and preparing to strike. He knows Victor is waking up. He knows Victor is choosing.

And he will be ready.

"I believe in you," she says.

He closes his eyes and nods. When he opens them there is no evidence of fear in them. There is something else, something harder, something more certain. Then he says. "Stay with me."

"I'm not going anywhere."

---

VICTOR

He steps into the center of the room. Amelia follows him. She has no shoes on and her dark hair is in tangles but her eyes are steady and certain.

The bond hums between them. Warm. Strong. But beneath the warmth, something else. Something waiting. Something that has never been asked to do this before. The Mate Bond was designed to connect. To bind. To bridge. It was never designed to protect. To shield. To sacrifice.

But it has to be now.

He closes his eyes. Places his palms flat against his chest. Feels the bond humming beneath his skin. Feels Amelia on the other side, solid, warm, present. The pull of the Hollow King is outside the wards. He can feel it through the bond, a cold draft, a pressure, a hunger. The creature is testing the boundary. Finding cracks. Preparing to push through.

He cannot stop it by holding the door shut. He has learned that. He has spent every night since the ceremony bracing against the pull, holding the door closed, but the Hollow King is patient. The Hollow King is strong. He will keep coming. And every time, Victor will have to hold harder. Until he cannot hold anymore.

So he stops holding onto the door and just opens it.

The bond flows through him like a river or a flood. It pours into his chest through his lungs and down his spine, into his hands and feet, then to his veins and he gasps. He can't take it anymore and his knees buckle but Amelia is there to catch him.

"It's too much," he says. "Amelia. It's — it's too much — "

"I'm here," she says. "I'm here. You're not alone."

She touches his forehead to hers. Her hands find his shoulders. Her eyes lock onto his. The bond connects them, not just the space between, but the space through. Inside. She can feel that she is not on the other side anymore. She is actually inside of the bond. She is with Victor, pulling and pushing and channeling.

And the transformation begins.

It is not a wolf shift, its not even a human shift. It is something somewhere in between. His bones do not crack, muscles don't expand. But there is something that is much deeper that changes. The bond flows through him and his skin begins to glow in a faint silver light. It looks like it is rippling like water. His eyes then open and they are no longer his deep gray, they are silver and glowing. unnaturally bright.

Amelia gasps. "Victor."

His voice comes out in a different tone that is not human, and not wolf. Its a frequency that feels like it vibrates in her chest, maybe even in her bones, in the bond itself. Its not really a word, more like a feeling. a command or declaration.

He is shifting, But not into a wolf, into something else, something much older. Something the Mate Bond was meant to create but has never really had to. He's shifting into a vessel, a conduit. A living channel for the bond's power. The Moon Goddess did not design him to do this. But she gave him the bond. And the bond gave him the choice.

He makes it.

The silver light spreads throughout his body, Its moves from his chest to his arms, then moves to his legs and face. It pulses with his heartbeat. Each beat sends a wave of light through his body, through the bond, through Amelia. She feels it, a warmth so intense it almost burns. The pain is real. His body is being stretched, opened, remade. His bones hum. His muscles spasm. His skin ripples with light. But he does not stop. He cannot stop. He has made his choice.

Amelia can feel it through the bond. She isn't just watching it happen. She feels it on the inside. She feels the silver light flowing through him. She feels the pain. She feels the strength. She feels the sacrifice. He is putting himself between the Hollow King and everyone he loves. He is making himself a living shield. And he is doing it for her. For the pack. For the Moon Goddess. For the bond itself.

She does not let go.

She channels back.

Her own power flows through the bond. but is not like his, no. It isn't silver, or light. She's a human so her power is smaller. It is different, much softer, But real no less. It is warm made of love and of stubborness, and fierce refusal to lose the person standing in front of her. It pours through the bond into him. It fuels him, gives him the strength he needs. She is not a warrior or shifter. She doesn't have magic. But she is here. Present. Connected. And she is not letting go.

---

The Hollow King strikes.

He feels it through the bond, a wave of darkness, a pressure so intense it nearly knocks him to his knees. The creature is outside the wards. He can feel him pressing against the boundary. The wards shudder. Cracks form. The Hollow King is strong. Stronger than Victor expected. Stronger than Josiah predicted. He is pushing through. Breaking through. The wards will not hold.

But Victor does not need the wards to hold.

He opens the door.

The darkness hits him. The vast coldness of the Hollow King's presence slams into the barrier they have created. Victor and Amelia stand shoulder to shoulder in the center of the room. His eyes are silver. His skin glows. His hands are raised, palms outward, channeling the bond. Amelia stands beside him. Her eyes are closed. Her hands are pressed to his chest. Her breathing is steady. Her voice is a whisper. Not words. Just his name.

Victor. Victor. Victor.

A mantra. A prayer. A heartbeat.

The Hollow King strikes. The darkness crashes against the silver barrier like a wave against a cliff. The impact is immense. Victor's knees buckle. Amelia's grip on his chest tightens. Her fingers dig into his skin.

The barrier holds.

The Hollow King's darkness recoils. For a moment, there is silence. Then the creature screams, a sound that echoes through the ward, not a physical scream. A psychic one. A shriek of rage and pain and something else. Something Victor cannot name. But he knows it. He felt it before. In the war room. In Josiah's eyes. In the pack's fear.

It is fear. The Hollow King is afraid.

Victor pushes harder through the bond. Amelia stands beside him. The barrier holds.

The Hollow King's darkness retreats. The creature flees.

Victor feels it, the presence disappearing. The pressure lifting. The cold fading. The bond settling. Warm. Steady. Whole. Stronger than ever.

He drops to his knees. The silver light fades from his skin. His eyes return to gray. His body is shaking. His hands tremble. His breath comes in gasps. He is exhausted. Drained. But alive.

Amelia drops beside him. Her hands find his face. Her thumbs press against his cheeks. His skin is warm. His eyes are tired. But he is alive.

"You did it," she says.

He looks at her with wet, grey tired eyes. But he's alive. and says. "We did it"

---

AMELIA

She stays on her knees beside him. Her hands still on his face. Her thumbs tracing his cheekbones. His skin is warm. His breathing is uneven. He is exhausted.

But alive.

The barrier held, The Hollow King is gone and the wards are intact. More importantly, the pact is safe.

Victor closes his eyes. Nods once. Slowly. His hands find her wrists. His grip is weak. Trembling. But present. Solid. Real.

"I'm here," he says. "I'm not going anywhere. Not after what we are."

She leans her forehead against his. Closes her eyes. Breathes. The bond hums between them. Warm. Strong. Unbroken.

And for the first time since the Hollow King arrived, Amelia believes, truly believes, that they can win this war.

Together.



\pagebreak

# Chapter 37: The Dawn

AMELIA

She wakes up to a peacefully quiet room.

The room has a soft morning light from the sun. You can see the dust hanging in the air. The compound is silent, almost too silent. The remanence of the Hollow King and the survivors.

Victor is laying beside her sleeping soundly. His breathing is slow. Uneven. His face is pressed into her shoulder. His arm is wrapped around her waist. His hand is curled against her ribs. He looks smaller in sleep. The silver light is gone from his skin. His eyes are gray.

She doesnt want to move and leave this moment.

The bond is still humming between them. It feels warm and strong. The barrier held in place, the Hollow King is gone. She can feel that the cold presence that was pressing against the wards is no longer there and The pack and safe.

But she knows this is not the end.

She closes her eyes. Breathes. Feels Victor's heartbeat against her back. Steady. Slow. Alive.

The compound begins to stir. Voices can be heard in the corridors. Footsteps and whispers are heard as pack members are checking on each other. They are assessing the damage, tending to wounds, all reminding her that this is the morning after the battle.

She stays still until she cannot.

Victor shifts. Mumbles. His arm tightens around her. His face presses deeper into her shoulder. For a second, she thinks he will fall back asleep.

Then he opens his eyes.

"Morning," he says. His voice is rough. Tired.

"Morning," she says.

He does not move. His arm stays around her waist. His breathing stays slow. He is not getting up. Not yet.

"Victor?"

He closes his eyes then says "I am here."

"Victor, you need to — " She touches his arm. "Josiah is going to want to know what happened last night. The pack is going to want to know what happened. You need to — "

He opens his eyes. His eyes are steady but tired. "I will go when I am ready."

She nods. Does not push. He just channeled a Mate Bond through his own body, became a living shield, and held a barrier against the Hollow King's full assault. He is exhausted. So she lets him rest.

But the compound is waking up. Voices are getting louder. Someone is shouting. Not in panic. In urgency.

Victor sits up all of a sudden. No longer looking exhausted. In its place is the Alpha everyone knows him to be. His jaw is set. His eyes are focused. He looks at her. "Stay here."

"Victor — "

"Stay here." He kisses her forehead and says. "I will be back."

He gets up, gets dressed quickly, and is out the door in seconds.

She stays in bed and listens to the voices as they get louder in the corridors. These are voices she does not recognize, pack members she has not met. She hears shouting, and crying. Others are ordering other members around.

She gets up. Dresses quickly. Pulls her hair back and walks to the door.

Stops.

She can hear Victor's voice over the others. Calm. Firm. In command.

But underneath it, something else. Something she recognizes through the bond. Worry. Fear. Concern. Not for himself. For someone else.

She opens the door.

---

VICTOR

He walks into the corridor. The compound is a wreck. The wards held, but the impact left its mark, shattered glass, cracked walls, scorch marks on the floor. Pack members are everywhere. Some are tending wounds. Others are checking damage. Others are standing in groups, their faces are pale and they are whispering.

Victor steps into the chaos. His Alpha presence cuts through the noise. Voices drop. Eyes turn toward him. He walks through the compound like a general surveying a battlefield, assessing, calculating, making decisions.

Perseus is in the healing tent. Surrounded by wounded. Seven dead. He saw the battle through the bond. He knows what happened. His face is grim. He nods at Victor. No words needed.

David is pacing. Angry. Relieved. Terrified. All at once. "The wards held. I felt it. I felt the barrier and it — " He stops. Shakes his head. "You did it. You actually did it."

"I told you," Victor says. Calm. Steady. "We do this together."

The exhaustion of carrying it all alone. And beneath all of it, love. For her. For the pack. For a future he does not know if he deserves.

It crashes into her like a wave. She gasps. Tears prick her eyes. She has never felt anything like it. It is too much. It is everything.

She lets him feel her back. Without thinking. Without planning. She lets him see it all. The loneliness before him. The certainty after him. The way she touches her moon tattoo when she is sad. The way she cannot sleep without hearing his voice in her head. The way she has loved him since the moment he looked at her like she mattered.

He stops breathing. His arms tighten around her. His grip is desperate. Clinging. She can feel him breaking open, the walls coming down piece by piece, letting her in, letting her see the man behind the Alpha.

When the wave passes, he is still holding her. Still breathing. But his grip is different. Looser. Relaxed. He is no longer fighting himself. He is no longer fighting her. He is just, here. With her. Present.

"I love you," he says again. And this time, it is not a statement. It is a vow.

---

VICTOR

He does not remember the last time he let someone see him like this. Like he is not the Alpha. Not the warrior. Not the man who holds everything together. Like he is just Victor. Scared. Tired. Human.

She sees all of it. Every part of him. And she does not look away. She pulls him closer. Kisses his forehead. Kisses his lips. Kisses his cheek. Her hands in his hair. Her body warm against his. Her heartbeat steady against his chest.

"I have you," she says. "I am not going anywhere."

He closes his eyes. Breathes. For a long moment, there is only the sound of their breathing. Her heartbeat. The quiet of the morning.

Then she shifts. Away from him. Sits up on the edge of the bed. Rubs her face with both hands.

"I need to tell you something," she says. "The thing I said I would tell you. Yesterday. Today."

He sits up. Watches her. She looks pale. Tired. But there is something else in her face. Something he cannot name. Wonder. Fear. Hope. The kind of fear that is not about loss.

"Okay," he says. "Tell me."

She looks at him. Gray eyes wet. Honest. "It is about us."

He stills. "What is it?"

She takes a breath. Fingers twist in the sheets. "I have been feeling things. Since the battle. My body aches. My head spins. I feel like I have been running for weeks. And I have been — " She stops. Swallows. Blinks. Swallows again. "I think something has been growing inside me. For a while."

He stares at her. The words do not compute. Not yet. They float above his head. Unreachable. Unclear.

"Growing," he says.

She nods. "I do not know what it is. But I think I need to find out."

He holds her hand. Squeezes. "We will find out. Together. Today. I will take you to Josiah. To Emila. To anyone who can help."

She smiles. A small, uncertain smile. "Okay."

He pulls her into his arms. Holds her tight. And for the first time in his life, Victor does not feel the weight of the world on his shoulders. He feels something else. Something warm. Something alive.

Something that does not need to be carried alone.

---

AMELIA

She falls back asleep in his arms. His body is warm against hers. His heartbeat steady against her back. The bond hums between them, quiet, comfortable. Like a fire in a cold room.

She dreams. Not of the Hollow King. Not of battles. Of something else. Something small. Something new. A heartbeat. Not hers. Not his. Neither of theirs. But both of theirs.

She does not know what it means. Not yet. But she feels it, a thread of hope, thin and fragile, stretching into the future.

And she holds onto it.

When she wakes, the sun is higher. Victor is awake. Watching her. His eyes are warm. Fond. Tired. But present.

"You can tell me," he says. "Whenever you are ready."

She smiles. "I know."

She reaches across the bed. Takes his hand. Squeezes. "I love you."

"I love you," he says. "More than I knew I could."

She kisses him. Slow. Soft. Sure. No bonds. No magic. No goddess. Just them. Two people who have fought for each other. Who have chosen each other. Who have seen each other at their most vulnerable and have not looked away.

And that is enough.



\pagebreak

# Chapter 40: The Horizon

---

# Epilogue

AMELIA

Weeks have passed. The pack is rebuilding. The barrier is strong. The Hollow King is gone, not defeated, but pushed back across the Veil. The Blackwood Compound has never felt more alive.

Victor and Amelia are bonded, fully and completely.

They are in the compound's kitchen in the early morning. She is making tea. He is sharpening his knives. Neither of them is talking. He reaches across the table and takes her hand. She reaches back. They both smile, not because they have to, but because they want to.

This is what they fought for.

She sets down her mug. Places her hand over his. The smile on her face is different now, softer, a little uncertain, like she has been carrying something and is finally ready to set it down.

"I have something to tell you," she says.

Victor stills. "What is it?"

She takes a breath. Looks at him. "I'm pregnant."

The silence stretches. The knives on the table catch the morning light. She takes a breath and tells him everything she has been carrying for weeks: the nausea, the fatigue, the fear, the hope, the love, the terrifying, beautiful certainty that something new is growing inside her.

And Victor — Victor feels it all. The weight of it in her voice. The tremor in her hands. Every spike of fear. Every quiet, terrified joy. His eyes go wide. His hand closes over hers on the table.

"I told you," she says. "A little while ago."

Victor stares at her. His mouth opens. Closes. Opens again. For the Alpha who never hesitates, who never stammers, who plans every scenario and calculates every outcome, he has nothing. No words. No strategy. Just his hand over hers.

"A baby," he says.

She nods. "A baby."

He looks at her. Really looks at her. Not with the Alpha's eyes. Not with the mate's bond. With his own. And then he pulls her out of her chair, into his arms, and she laughs, surprised, bright, real, and he spins her once, twice, and sets her down and presses his forehead to hers and whispers, "A baby."

She wraps her arms around his neck. "You're happy?"

He closes his eyes. "I am. I am. I am."

She rests her head on his shoulder and breathes. "We're going to be parents."

"We're going to be parents."

He holds her. Long. Tight. Like he's memorizing the shape of her in his arms. And when he finally lets go, he pulls out his chair, sits across from her, takes her hand again, and says the quietest thing either of them has ever said:

"Our pup."

She smiles. The kind of smile that starts in the chest and spreads outward, slow and certain and full of everything they're afraid to hope for but can't stop believing in. "Ours."

---

VICTOR

He sits across from her. Her hand in his. The morning light falls across the table. Dust motes float in the air. The compound is alive outside, pack members training, children running, dogs barking. The sounds of a living, breathing pack.

A pup.

Their pup.

He feels the weight of what is about to happen, warm, real, unbreakable, and for the first time in his life, Victor understands what it means to have a future. Not just survival. Not just holding the line. A future.

He looks at her. Amelia. His mate. The woman who walked into shifter territory with nothing but a bag of herbs and a crescent moon tattoo on her wrist. The woman who healed his warriors when no one else would. The woman who stood beside him when the Hollow King came. The woman who never let him go.

A baby.

His baby.

The weight of it presses on him, not heavy, but real. Solid. Present. He feels it in his chest. In the way his hand closes around hers without thinking. He is going to be a father.

And he is not afraid.

---

AMELIA

Weeks later, the pack is in better shape. The barrier holds. The Hollow King has not returned. The Blackwood compound is busy, pack members training, healers tending to injuries, children playing in the courtyard. Life is returning to normal. A new kind of normal, but a normal nonetheless.

Perseus comes to the compound gate with a message from the southern border. He finds Victor in the war room, looking over maps and patrol routes. Perseus hands him the letter without a word.

Victor opens it. Reads it. His expression does not change, but Perseus notices the way his fingers tighten around the paper.

"What is it?" Victor asks.

Perseus exhales slowly. "Hanley found something on their territory. Something that doesn't smell like wolf."

Victor's eyebrows rise. "What does it smell like?"

Perseus hesitates. Looks at him. "Panther."

The room goes quiet. Victor puts the letter down. Stands. Walks to the window. Looks south.

A panther pack.

One of the six shifter types Aurela mentioned. Not wolf. Not fox. Not bear. Panther. A different kind of shifter. A different kind of pack.

Victor does not say anything for a long moment. Then he turns back to Perseus.

"Did Gage see them?"

Perseus nods. "Hanley confirmed it. A small group of panthers, maybe thirty. They've been moving through the southern territories for months. Gage wants to reach out. He thinks they might be worth talking to."

Victor nods. "I will go."

Perseus stares at him. "You? The Alpha of the Blackwood Pack? You want to walk across hostile territory to meet strangers?"

Victor smiles. "I've learned something. Connection is stronger than fear. If we want to stop the Hollow King, really stop him, we need more than wolves. We need a coalition. All six types. All six mates."

Perseus considers this. Nods slowly. "When do we leave?"

"Tomorrow."

Perseus leaves. Victor stays in the war room. Looks at the map. South. To the panther pack. To the next mate. To the next bridge.

He is not afraid. He has his mate. He has his pack.

And he knows this is just the beginning.

---

AMELIA

She finds him in the war room later. He is still looking at the map. His jaw is set. His eyes are distant. She does not speak. Just stands beside him. Her hand on his shoulder.

He turns. Looks at her. Smiles.

"You're leaving," she says.

He nods. "Tomorrow. Perseus is going with me. We're going to meet a panther pack."

She nods. "How do you feel?"

He takes her hand. Squeezes. "Ready."

She smiles. "Good."

She kisses him. Slow. Soft. Sure. No magic. No goddess. Just them. Two people who have fought for each other. Who have chosen each other. Who have seen each other at their most vulnerable and have not looked away.

She steps back. Looks at her stomach. At the life growing inside her.

"I'm going to be a mother," she says.

He steps closer. Wraps his arms around her. One hand on her stomach. The other on her back. Presses his forehead to hers.

"Our pup," he says.

She smiles. "Ours."

They stand together in the war room. Looking at the map. Looking at the future. The Hollow King is out there. The Veil is still thin. The war is not over. But they are not afraid.

They have each other. They have a pack. They have a future.

And that is enough.
